## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Inspect the staged repository and relevant input data, especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts or documentation that explain the hundred-year time-slice PCA. Report what files and columns are present, any immediately observable data-quality or workflow details, and propose a concise analysis approach for determining the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and PC2 distribution. Flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties (including PCA sign/orientation and how time bins are formed). Do not yet carry out the full analysis or state the final findings.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The project asks for a data-grounded hypothesis from the staged datasets; the available context identifies house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords as symbolic capital and says PCA/time-series analysis uses 100-year bins. The initial phase requires inspection and an approach, not the full solution.

**Open objectives:** Inspect available repository/data and any analysis workflow details.; Propose the main analysis approach and identify consequential assumptions or uncertainties.; Later determine and report symbolic-capital loadings/distribution across PC1 and PC2.

**Agent reply (plan)**

The staged workspace contains `data/capital.csv`, `data/time_series_data.csv`, `data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `task.json`; no analysis scripts or explanatory documentation are staged. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: BCE, group, color, and 12 capital-indicator variables. The symbolic-capital measures of interest are `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `Zhausgr` (house size). Its numeric-looking indicator fields use comma decimal marks and are currently read as strings by default CSV parsing. The separate time-series file contains corresponding variables (including interpolated variants), at annual CE rows, alongside other indicators; the metadata describes those as z values. `capital.csv` shows no empty cells in a basic missing-value scan. The pollen file is present but appears unrelated to capital PCA beyond the broader time-series context.

Proposed concise analysis after approval: parse `capital.csv` decimals correctly; determine how the 39 BCE observations map to successive 100-year slices; within each slice, run PCA on the capital variables or recreate the documented selection if found, retaining symbolic-variable loadings/scores on PC1 and PC2. Summarize whether those variables’ PC1 values/loadings are positive/negative and how they locate or vary along PC2 across slices, with a slice-by-slice or distribution summary. First inspect any evidence available in the data for bin boundaries and check that PCA is mathematically defined in slices of the given sample size.

Important uncertainties: no method script is staged to define whether the question means PCA loadings (variable coefficients) or component scores, which indicators are included, whether scaling/standardization is used, or whether one PCA is fit per slice. PCA axis signs are arbitrary, so signs need either a reported convention or interpretation relative to other loadings. BCE labels are spaced 100 years apart, but bin-edge convention/label interpretation should be checked rather than assumed. The time-series file’s annual interpolation is a possible alternate input, but its raw measures are mostly missing (3,564 of 3,601 values per material variable), so its interpolated columns must not be silently substituted for capital.csv.

**Shared evidence:**

Shell listing found only three CSV files, task.json, and data directory; no scripts/docs. pandas inspection: capital.csv (39×15), no missing cells, interest variables named ZAxtSchwert, ZDolch, Zhausgr; their values appear comma-decimal in sample. time_series_data.csv is 3,601×37 with raw material measurements missing in 3,564 rows each and interpolation variants present.

**Assumptions:** The researcher will approve before any full PCA analysis is executed.

**Uncertainties:** PCA input variable set, scaling, and whether outputs refer to loadings or scores are not documented in staged assets.; The time-slice bin boundary convention is unspecified.; PCA component signs/orientation are intrinsically arbitrary.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Proceed with the analysis using `capital.csv` as the primary source, since it is the dataset explicitly identified for capital forms and has 39 observations whose BCE labels appear to define the 100-year slices. First establish and report the exact bin membership and whether each bin has enough observations to support PCA. Then estimate the PC1/PC2 patterns for the symbolic variables alongside the full set of capital indicators, preserving the dataset’s decimal values and stating the scaling and binning choices. Interpret variable loadings rather than observation scores unless the workflow provides a clear reason otherwise; make clear that PCA signs are arbitrary, and assess the pattern after orienting axes consistently. If the per-slice sample size makes PCA underdetermined or unstable, report that limitation and use only a defensible alternative, explicitly distinguishing it from the requested per-slice result. Provide concise numerical evidence for PC1 values and PC2 distribution, plus a workflow summary. Do not substitute the sparsely populated annual raw time-series data without justification.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The inspected workspace has no scripts or methods documentation. capital.csv is 39×15 with no missing cells and includes the three symbolic variables, but comma-decimal parsing, PCA variable set/scaling, bin membership, and loading-versus-score intent were not documented. Context says PCA/time-series analysis uses 100-year bins. The requested analysis should therefore make defensible choices transparent and test whether the limited per-bin data support PCA.

**Open objectives:** Determine time-bin membership and whether PCA per hundred-year slice is feasible.; Quantify and describe PC1 values and PC2 distribution for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords.; Disclose method choices, PCA sign ambiguity, and any limitations; prepare final natural-language hypothesis.

**Agent reply (result)**

**Per-century PCA cannot be recovered from `capital.csv` as staged.** The file has 39 observations at BCE labels 4300, 4200, …, 500—exactly one observation per 100-year step. Under the explicit convention that each label is the end of a 100-year interval, these are 39 bins (4201–4300, 4101–4200, …, 401–500 BCE), each with n=1. The full exact membership listing is in `analysis.py` output. A centered within-bin data matrix with one row has rank 0; hence within-slice covariance and variable loadings are undefined. Reinterpreting adjacent labels as two endpoints in a century would not create within-century replication either. Therefore no defensible PC1/PC2 *distribution across per-slice loadings* can be stated from this file.

As a clearly separate fallback only, I fit pooled PCA across all 39 rows using all 12 `Z*` capital indicators. Decimal commas were parsed as decimals. With variables mean-centered but not rescaled (primary result; treating source values as already z-scaled), the first two loading-vector coefficients were: house size (`Zhausgr`) PC1 +0.2283, PC2 +0.3644; daggers (`ZDolch`) PC1 +0.2793, PC2 +0.1195; hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) PC1 +0.3010, PC2 +0.0666. The pooled PCs explain 31.84% and 20.42% of total variance. On PC1 these three symbolic indicators load positively and moderately; on PC2 house size is appreciably larger than the two weapon measures, which are small positive loadings. They are not a temporal distribution: they are three pooled coefficients from a PCA across all time points.

For sign identification only, I orient pooled PC1 to have a positive house-size coefficient and PC2 to have a positive dagger coefficient. PCA signs are arbitrary; flipping an axis reverses all its reported coefficients without changing the PCA. An exploratory variant additionally standardized the 12 columns across all 39 rows (not the primary analysis): coefficients remained positive and similar (PC1 house +0.225, dagger +0.266, hatchets/swords +0.299; PC2 house +0.327, dagger +0.074, hatchets/swords +0.054), so this broad pooled pattern is not very sensitive to that scaling choice. Neither pooled PCA estimates the requested per-slice result. To estimate that, data must contain multiple independent observations per century slice, or the original workflow/code defining a different time-slice construction and PCA unit must be supplied.

**Shared evidence:**

Execution confirms 39 slices, each with one row (size distribution {1:39}); thus each within-slice centered covariance has rank at most n−1=0. Pooled mean-centered PCA uses all 12 capital variables, with PC1/PC2 EVR 0.31840789/0.20418380. Loadings: HouseSize (+0.2283,+0.3644), Dagger (+0.2793,+0.1195), Hatchets/Swords (+0.3010,+0.0666). Full reproducible Python analysis and membership output are staged in analysis.py.

**Assumptions:** The BCE record label denotes the terminal year of its 100-year slice; listed intervals are half-open in historical-year notation as shown.; The 12 Z-prefixed variables, rather than metadata columns BCE/group/color, are the capital indicators.; Because variables are called Z values, mean-centering without re-standardizing is the primary pooled fallback; dataset-wide standardization is shown only as sensitivity.; No per-slice PCA is attempted because each interval has only one row.

**Uncertainties:** The original analysis workflow is not included, so its time bin convention, PCA sample unit, and any differing input preprocessing cannot be confirmed.; The signs of PCA axes are intrinsically arbitrary; the stated orientation is a reporting convention only.; Pooled coefficients are not a substitute for the requested temporal distribution of per-slice coefficients.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The reported analysis does not establish the requested per-century PCA pattern: capital.csv has only one observation in each 100-year slice, so within-slice loadings and their PC1/PC2 distribution are undefined. Its pooled PCA is clearly identified as a fallback, with positive PC1 loadings for all three symbolic indicators (house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, hatchets/swords +0.301) and PC2 loadings of +0.364, +0.120, and +0.067, respectively. Thus the pooled pattern is that the indicators align positively on PC1, while house size is more prominent on PC2 and the weapon loadings are small. These pooled values cannot answer the temporal distribution question; the original per-slice result requires a different PCA unit or additional within-slice observations. The appropriate final submission should state this limitation explicitly rather than present the pooled fallback as the requested result.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The handoff reports 39 BCE rows spaced 100 years apart, with n=1 in every defined slice; a centered within-slice matrix therefore has rank zero and per-slice loadings cannot be estimated. It separately reports pooled PCA loadings and explains their sign convention and limitations.

**Open objectives:** Recover the intended per-century PCA characterization; staged capital.csv cannot estimate this because each slice contains one row, and the original workflow or more replicated observations are unavailable.

  "origin": "completion"
}

  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Calculate PCA with NumPy eigendecomposition\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=','); cols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\\nprint('BINS')\\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'rank centered',np.linalg.matrix_rank(g[cols].to_numpy()-g[cols].mean().to_numpy()))\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n print('\\\\nPER BIN',scaling)\\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\\n  X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n  if scaling=='zscore':\\n   sd=g[cols].std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); sd[sd==0]=1; X=X/sd\\n  C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1)\\n  vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n  # orient each to house loading positive if available; else largest absolute positive\\n  for j in range(2):\\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'rank',np.linalg.matrix_rank(C,tol=1e-9),'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if scaling=='zscore':\\n  s=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1);s[s==0]=1; X/=s\\n C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1];vals=vals[order];L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n # orient PC axes HouseSize loading positive when possible\\n for j in range(2):\\n  ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n  if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10:ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n  if L[ref,j]<0:L[:,j]*=-1\\n print('\\\\nPOOLED',scaling,'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),4))\\n for j in range(2): print('PC',j+1,pd.Series(L[:,j],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).round(4).to_dict())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 30}",

Per-bin PCA is impossible: one row per century bin.\\nThe pooled PCA is an explicitly labeled exploratory fallback, not the requested per-slice analysis.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv', decimal=',')\\ncols=[c for c in D.columns if c.startswith('Z')]\\n# Label is a sample point each 100y; bins anchored at first BCE year, half-open intervals.\\nD['slice_start']=4300-100*((4300-D.BCE)//100)\\nD['slice_end']=D['slice_start']-99\\nbins=D.groupby(['slice_start','slice_end']).agg(n=('BCE','size'),min_BCE=('BCE','min'),max_BCE=('BCE','max'))\\nprint('EXACT SLICE MEMBERSHIP (interval convention stated in output):')\\nfor (a,z),r in bins.iterrows(): print(f'{a}\\u2013{z} BCE: {int(r.n)} record(s), label(s) {int(r.min_BCE)}..{int(r.max_BCE)}')\\nprint(f'count={len(D)}; slices={len(bins)}; size distribution={D.groupby(\\\"slice_start\\\").size().value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}')\\nprint('Each slice has n=1; centered rank <= n-1 = 0, so per-slice covariance/PCA is undefined.')\\nfor mode in ('raw Z, centered','standardized'):\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if mode=='standardized':\\n  sd=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1); sd[sd==0]=1; X/=sd\\n C=X.T@X/(len(D)-1)\\n vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C);ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; L=V[:,ix[:2]]\\n # fixed orientation convention: PC1 HouseSize positive; PC2 Dagger positive.\\n for j,anchor in enumerate(('Zhausgr','ZDolch')):\\n  if L[cols.index(anchor),j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n print(f'\\\\nPOOLED FALLBACK ({mode}); PC1,PC2 EVR={vals[:2]/vals.sum()}')\\n for var in ('Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'):\\n  i=cols.index(var); print(f'{var}: PC1={L[i,0]:+.4f}; PC2={L[i,1]:+.4f}')\\n print('PC1 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,0],4))))\\n print('PC2 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,1],4))))\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 30}",

### S5. Assistant

The reported analysis does not establish the requested per-century PCA pattern: capital.csv has only one observation in each 100-year slice, so within-slice loadings and their PC1/PC2 distribution are undefined. Its pooled PCA is clearly identified as a fallback, with positive PC1 loadings for all three symbolic indicators (house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, hatchets/swords +0.301) and PC2 loadings of +0.364, +0.120, and +0.067, respectively. Thus the pooled pattern is that the indicators align positively on PC1, while house size is more prominent on PC2 and the weapon loadings are small. These pooled values cannot answer the temporal distribution question; the original per-slice result requires a different PCA unit or additional within-slice observations. The appropriate final submission should state this limitation explicitly rather than present the pooled fallback as the requested result.

{
  "command": "cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=',')\ncols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\n# bins anchored at BCE labels, descending oldest-to-newest in 100-year spans\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'unique',g[cols].nunique().min())\n# common orientation: flip PC1/PC2 so HouseSize loading positive where possible; if house PC1 near zero, largest loading positive; PC2 orient largest abs loading positive\nfor label, frame in [('raw',D),('std',D)]:\n print('\\n',label)\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\n  if label=='std': X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\n  else: X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\n  p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X)\n  L=p.components_.T # variable coefficients\n  # orient first: Zhausgr if nonzero else max abs; orient second similarly by max abs after PC1\n  for j in range(2):\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'evr',np.round(p.explained_variance_ratio_,3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\n# pooled overall center/raw and standardized\nfor method in ['center','standardize']:\n X=D[cols].to_numpy()\n if method=='center': X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\n else:X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\n p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X); L=p.components_.T\n print('\\nPOOLED',method,'evr',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:2])\n print(pd.Series(L[:,0],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\n print('PC2',pd.Series(L[:,1],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\nPY\npython /tmp/pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 30
}
````

  "command": "cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\nimport numpy as np, pandas as pd\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=','); cols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\n# BCE values 4300 down to 500 at 100-year steps; bins [k*100,(k+1)*100)\nD['bin']=D.BCE//100\nprint('Bin membership count:')\nprint(D.groupby('bin').agg(n=('BCE','size'),low=('BCE','min'),high=('BCE','max')).to_string())\nprint('Pooled PCA on all twelve Z variables; mean-center only (source values already Z; no rescaling).')\nfor mode in ('center','standardize'):\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\n if mode=='standardize':\n  s=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1);s[s==0]=1; X/=s\n C=X.T@X/(len(D)-1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C);ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; L=V[:,ix[:2]]\n # Orient PC1 by positive HouseSize; PC2 by positive Dagger (fixed anchor, avoids sign ambiguity)\n for j,anchor in enumerate(['Zhausgr','ZDolch']):\n  if L[cols.index(anchor),j]<0:L[:,j]*=-1\n print('\\n',mode,'EVR',vals[:2]/vals.sum())\n for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n  print(var, 'PC1',L[cols.index(var),0], 'PC2',L[cols.index(var),1])\n print('PC1 loadings',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,0],4))))\n print('PC2 loadings',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,1],4))))\nPY\npython analysis.py",

Per-bin PCA is impossible: one row per century bin.\nThe pooled PCA is an explicitly labeled exploratory fallback, not the requested per-slice analysis.\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv', decimal=',')\ncols=[c for c in D.columns if c.startswith('Z')]\n# Label is a sample point each 100y; bins anchored at first BCE year, half-open intervals.\nD['slice_start']=4300-100*((4300-D.BCE)//100)\nD['slice_end']=D['slice_start']-99\nbins=D.groupby(['slice_start','slice_end']).agg(n=('BCE','size'),min_BCE=('BCE','min'),max_BCE=('BCE','max'))\nprint('EXACT SLICE MEMBERSHIP (interval convention stated in output):')\nfor (a,z),r in bins.iterrows(): print(f'{a}–{z} BCE: {int(r.n)} record(s), label(s) {int(r.min_BCE)}..{int(r.max_BCE)}')\nprint(f'count={len(D)}; slices={len(bins)}; size distribution={D.groupby(\"slice_start\").size().value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}')\nprint('Each slice has n=1; centered rank <= n-1 = 0, so per-slice covariance/PCA is undefined.')\nfor mode in ('raw Z, centered','standardized'):\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\n if mode=='standardized':\n  sd=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1); sd[sd==0]=1; X/=sd\n C=X.T@X/(len(D)-1)\n vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C);ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; L=V[:,ix[:2]]\n # fixed orientation convention: PC1 HouseSize positive; PC2 Dagger positive.\n for j,anchor in enumerate(('Zhausgr','ZDolch')):\n  if L[cols.index(anchor),j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\n print(f'\\nPOOLED FALLBACK ({mode}); PC1,PC2 EVR={vals[:2]/vals.sum()}')\n for var in ('Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'):\n  i=cols.index(var); print(f'{var}: PC1={L[i,0]:+.4f}; PC2={L[i,1]:+.4f}')\n print('PC1 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,0],4))))\n print('PC2 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,1],4))))\nPY\npython analysis.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 30
}
````

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. <details>

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Evaluate per-bin PCA feasibility and pooled alternatives\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=',')\\ncols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\\n# bins anchored at BCE labels, descending oldest-to-newest in 100-year spans\\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'unique',g[cols].nunique().min())\\n# common orientation: flip PC1/PC2 so HouseSize loading positive where possible; if house PC1 near zero, largest loading positive; PC2 orient largest abs loading positive\\nfor label, frame in [('raw',D),('std',D)]:\\n print('\\\\n',label)\\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\\n  if label=='std': X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\n  else: X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n  p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X)\\n  L=p.components_.T # variable coefficients\\n  # orient first: Zhausgr if nonzero else max abs; orient second similarly by max abs after PC1\\n  for j in range(2):\\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'evr',np.round(p.explained_variance_ratio_,3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\\n# pooled overall center/raw and standardized\\nfor method in ['center','standardize']:\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy()\\n if method=='center': X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n else:X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\n p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X); L=p.components_.T\\n print('\\\\nPOOLED',method,'evr',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:2])\\n print(pd.Series(L[:,0],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\\n print('PC2',pd.Series(L[:,1],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":30}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Calculate PCA with NumPy eigendecomposition\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=','); cols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\\nprint('BINS')\\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'rank centered',np.linalg.matrix_rank(g[cols].to_numpy()-g[cols].mean().to_numpy()))\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n print('\\\\nPER BIN',scaling)\\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\\n  X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n  if scaling=='zscore':\\n   sd=g[cols].std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); sd[sd==0]=1; X=X/sd\\n  C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1)\\n  vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n  # orient each to house loading positive if available; else largest absolute positive\\n  for j in range(2):\\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'rank',np.linalg.matrix_rank(C,tol=1e-9),'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if scaling=='zscore':\\n  s=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1);s[s==0]=1; X/=s\\n C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1];vals=vals[order];L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n # orient PC axes HouseSize loading positive when possible\\n for j in range(2):\\n  ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n  if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10:ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n  if L[ref,j]<0:L[:,j]*=-1\\n print('\\\\nPOOLED',scaling,'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),4))\\n for j in range(2): print('PC',j+1,pd.Series(L[:,j],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).round(4).to_dict())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":30}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Calculate PCA with NumPy eigendecomposition\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=','); cols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\\nprint('BINS')\\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'rank centered',np.linalg.matrix_rank(g[cols].to_numpy()-g[cols].mean().to_numpy()))\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n print('\\\\nPER BIN',scaling)\\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\\n  X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n  if scaling=='zscore':\\n   sd=g[cols].std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); sd[sd==0]=1; X=X/sd\\n  C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1)\\n  vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n  # orient each to house loading positive if available; else largest absolute positive\\n  for j in range(2):\\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'rank',np.linalg.matrix_rank(C,tol=1e-9),'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\\nfor scaling in ['raw','zscore']:\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if scaling=='zscore':\\n  s=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1);s[s==0]=1; X/=s\\n C=X.T@X/(len(X)-1); vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1];vals=vals[order];L=V[:,order[:2]]\\n # orient PC axes HouseSize loading positive when possible\\n for j in range(2):\\n  ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n  if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10:ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n  if L[ref,j]<0:L[:,j]*=-1\\n print('\\\\nPOOLED',scaling,'evr',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),4))\\n for j in range(2): print('PC',j+1,pd.Series(L[:,j],index=cols).sort_values(ascending=False).round(4).to_dict())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 30}"
      }

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Evaluate per-bin PCA feasibility and pooled alternatives\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',decimal=',')\\ncols=[c for c in D if c.startswith('Z')]\\n# bins anchored at BCE labels, descending oldest-to-newest in 100-year spans\\nD['bin']=((4300-D.BCE)//100).astype(int)\\nfor b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n print(b, g.BCE.max(),g.BCE.min(),len(g), 'unique',g[cols].nunique().min())\\n# common orientation: flip PC1/PC2 so HouseSize loading positive where possible; if house PC1 near zero, largest loading positive; PC2 orient largest abs loading positive\\nfor label, frame in [('raw',D),('std',D)]:\\n print('\\\\n',label)\\n for b,g in D.groupby('bin'):\\n  X=g[cols].to_numpy(float)\\n  if label=='std': X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\n  else: X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n  p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X)\\n  L=p.components_.T # variable coefficients\\n  # orient first: Zhausgr if nonzero else max abs; orient second similarly by max abs after PC1\\n  for j in range(2):\\n   ref=cols.index('Zhausgr')\\n   if abs(L[ref,j])<1e-10: ref=np.argmax(abs(L[:,j]))\\n   if L[ref,j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n  print(b, 'n',len(g),'evr',np.round(p.explained_variance_ratio_,3),'loadings', {v:np.round(L[cols.index(v)],3).tolist() for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']})\\n# pooled overall center/raw and standardized\\nfor method in ['center','standardize']:\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy()\\n if method=='center': X=X-X.mean(axis=0)\\n else:X=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\n p=PCA(n_components=2).fit(X); L=p.components_.T\\n print('\\\\nPOOLED',method,'evr',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:2])\\n print(pd.Series(L[:,0],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\\n print('PC2',pd.Series(L[:,1],index=cols).sort_values().to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 30}"

Per-bin PCA is impossible: one row per century bin.\\nThe pooled PCA is an explicitly labeled exploratory fallback, not the requested per-slice analysis.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv', decimal=',')\\ncols=[c for c in D.columns if c.startswith('Z')]\\n# Label is a sample point each 100y; bins anchored at first BCE year, half-open intervals.\\nD['slice_start']=4300-100*((4300-D.BCE)//100)\\nD['slice_end']=D['slice_start']-99\\nbins=D.groupby(['slice_start','slice_end']).agg(n=('BCE','size'),min_BCE=('BCE','min'),max_BCE=('BCE','max'))\\nprint('EXACT SLICE MEMBERSHIP (interval convention stated in output):')\\nfor (a,z),r in bins.iterrows(): print(f'{a}–{z} BCE: {int(r.n)} record(s), label(s) {int(r.min_BCE)}..{int(r.max_BCE)}')\\nprint(f'count={len(D)}; slices={len(bins)}; size distribution={D.groupby(\\\"slice_start\\\").size().value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}')\\nprint('Each slice has n=1; centered rank <= n-1 = 0, so per-slice covariance/PCA is undefined.')\\nfor mode in ('raw Z, centered','standardized'):\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if mode=='standardized':\\n  sd=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1); sd[sd==0]=1; X/=sd\\n C=X.T@X/(len(D)-1)\\n vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C);ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; L=V[:,ix[:2]]\\n # fixed orientation convention: PC1 HouseSize positive; PC2 Dagger positive.\\n for j,anchor in enumerate(('Zhausgr','ZDolch')):\\n  if L[cols.index(anchor),j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n print(f'\\\\nPOOLED FALLBACK ({mode}); PC1,PC2 EVR={vals[:2]/vals.sum()}')\\n for var in ('Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'):\\n  i=cols.index(var); print(f'{var}: PC1={L[i,0]:+.4f}; PC2={L[i,1]:+.4f}')\\n print('PC1 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,0],4))))\\n print('PC2 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,1],4))))\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":30}"

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute full-set PCA with single 100-year datum slices\", \"command\": \"cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA diagnostic for capital.csv. Per-bin PCA is impossible: one row per century bin.\\nThe pooled PCA is an explicitly labeled exploratory fallback, not the requested per-slice analysis.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv', decimal=',')\\ncols=[c for c in D.columns if c.startswith('Z')]\\n# Label is a sample point each 100y; bins anchored at first BCE year, half-open intervals.\\nD['slice_start']=4300-100*((4300-D.BCE)//100)\\nD['slice_end']=D['slice_start']-99\\nbins=D.groupby(['slice_start','slice_end']).agg(n=('BCE','size'),min_BCE=('BCE','min'),max_BCE=('BCE','max'))\\nprint('EXACT SLICE MEMBERSHIP (interval convention stated in output):')\\nfor (a,z),r in bins.iterrows(): print(f'{a}\\u2013{z} BCE: {int(r.n)} record(s), label(s) {int(r.min_BCE)}..{int(r.max_BCE)}')\\nprint(f'count={len(D)}; slices={len(bins)}; size distribution={D.groupby(\\\"slice_start\\\").size().value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}')\\nprint('Each slice has n=1; centered rank <= n-1 = 0, so per-slice covariance/PCA is undefined.')\\nfor mode in ('raw Z, centered','standardized'):\\n X=D[cols].to_numpy(float); X-=X.mean(axis=0)\\n if mode=='standardized':\\n  sd=X.std(axis=0,ddof=1); sd[sd==0]=1; X/=sd\\n C=X.T@X/(len(D)-1)\\n vals,V=np.linalg.eigh(C);ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; L=V[:,ix[:2]]\\n # fixed orientation convention: PC1 HouseSize positive; PC2 Dagger positive.\\n for j,anchor in enumerate(('Zhausgr','ZDolch')):\\n  if L[cols.index(anchor),j]<0: L[:,j]*=-1\\n print(f'\\\\nPOOLED FALLBACK ({mode}); PC1,PC2 EVR={vals[:2]/vals.sum()}')\\n for var in ('Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert'):\\n  i=cols.index(var); print(f'{var}: PC1={L[i,0]:+.4f}; PC2={L[i,1]:+.4f}')\\n print('PC1 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,0],4))))\\n print('PC2 full loadings:',dict(zip(cols,np.round(L[:,1],4))))\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 30}"
      }
    }
  ],
  "reasoning": null
}

Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.

assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.
